User: Welcome back. It is now 10/13/2026. Let's start up a new chat.

As I was testing, I found that there was a bit of aesthetic that I wanted to deal with a bit more within the 2D Local Map. As seen in the shared screenshots, we have a really simple case where a choice for a couple membranes to be a tiny bit taller could prevent the uneven top surface of the `packages/engine/src/` directory.

This is just something I noticed aesthetically, and it got me wondering: Since GPT-6 Astra is _miles_ ahead of competition on visualization and spatial understanding, perhaps you can help me with a session in which we iteratively look at different 2D Local Map views, analyze their screenshots, and see what we can do with the layout rules to bias them in favor of those more-aesthetically-pleasing layouts. And in my case, more-aesthetically-pleasing actually means "less work for the eye and brain to perform while presenting identical information". We want to make the picture look more coherent in more situations. You'll see that I've tried quite a bit of this iteration with Fable 5.1, but I'm not confident that it had the full ability to really intuit a few of these more truly aesthetic/taste preference.

Can we make it our goal today to further improve the legibility of the explorer to the end user? Yesterday's nit-driven-development session was PHENOMENAL.

Codex: Yes. I’ll read the recent sessions and yesterday’s capture, then inspect the Local Map behind these screenshots. The uneven membrane tops are a useful first case: we can test whether a little extra space makes the grouping easier to read, and check the same rule across other views before keeping it.

The geometry reveals a useful distinction: the existing rule charges for the **height of steps**, but not for how many separate steps the eye must follow. Flattening this small inner step can move the same height difference to an outer boundary, leaving the score unchanged. I’m preparing a comparison that keeps the cards and wires fixed so we can judge that difference directly.

function prose(text){let fence='';return text.split(/\\r?\\n/).filter(line=>{const m=line.match(/"'^'"\\s*("'`{3,}|~{3,})/);if(m){if(!fence)fence=m[1];else if(m[1][0]===fence[0]&&m[1].length>=fence.length)fence='"'';return false;}return "'!fence;}).join('"'\\n');}

Yes. Today’s goal is **less visual effort while presenting identical information**. The bootstrap and yesterday’s handoff are complete.

Your example exposes a blind spot: the existing rule penalizes the **height of steps**, but doesn’t distinguish several small steps from fewer larger ones. Increasing “Membrane Evenness” therefore doesn’t necessarily simplify the picture.

In a fresh generator view, expanding two enclosing membranes by 28 pixels removes the inner step. **Every card and wire path stays unchanged.**

| Current           | Illustrated alternative |
| ----------------- | ----------------------- |
| `Current outline` | `Simplified outline`    |

This is a visual experiment; product code hasn’t changed yet. The `probe record` preserves the measurements and limitations.

I recommend starting with a general preference for **removing unnecessary boundary turns using spare space**, then checking it across the warehouse, dense repository views, and C# estate—including motion during pinning.

The first design choice: **keep cards and wires fixed for this pass, or let simpler outlines compete with their placement immediately?** I recommend keeping them fixed first, so we can judge the outline improvement independently.

Handoff and evidence committed as `8d677211`; Markdown checks pass and the workspace is clean.

User: >The first design choice: **keep cards and wires fixed for this pass, or let simpler outlines compete with their placement immediately?** I recommend keeping them fixed first, so we can judge the outline improvement independently.

Since a competition system already exists, I suspect that it will be preferable to engage with this system, but also suspect this is another slider whose weight we can care about more or less, dialing to taste until a solid default is located. I wouldn't even be averse to letting users choose a "Compact" (strongly prefer space-saving) layout or "Geometric" (strongly prefer geometry evenness, element alignment at top or bottom, similarity of grid-like arrangements of nodes, etc.) layout. I bet we could tune the preferences to support one versus the other, and perhaps switching causes the layout bootstrapping to occur once again?

I found another gap in the existing competition: membrane evenness and row alignment influence each candidate’s placement, but their costs are omitted when choosing the winner.

I’ll make that scoring consistent, then add a tunable cost per boundary step and let the search try joining neighboring flat runs. Cards and routes can move when the combined score favors it. Changing a weight already restarts the search, so that mechanism also supports comparing Compact and Geometric settings.

The generator now settles at four boundary steps instead of seven, with the same wire-placement cost and picture size. The warehouse exposes a correctness issue: cached measurements predict a cheaper layout than the browser actually draws, causing repeated adoptions. I’m fixing that discrepancy and making the final browser measurement decide whether a candidate is accepted before judging the pictures.

The warehouse discrepancy comes from three concrete measurement errors: glyph margins distorted row spacing, duplicate symbol names could resolve to the wrong row, and file-level or asset anchors were missing from the cache. The cache is being changed to record the endpoints the renderer actually resolves, so the competition evaluates the connections people see.

The measurement fix holds: browser and headless results now agree exactly for the warehouse and estate.

The slider also demonstrates the tradeoff you described. After a generator re-layout, removing the remaining small step needs 14 extra pixels of picture height. At a weight of 50, compactness wins; at 100, the flatter boundary wins without increasing wire cost. I’m checking that transition visually.

# Part 2 (Separate chat, harness seemed to have trouble on the first one)

User: Apologies, it looked like the harness got a little wonky there. Needed to restart VSCode. Can you help reorient me? Where is this at?

> I recommend starting with a general preference for **removing unnecessary boundary turns using spare space**, then checking it across the warehouse, dense repository views, and C# estate—including motion during pinning.

Awesome. Wow fascinatingly the harness seems to have lost my last response too!

Nice job on keeping the record fresh!

```text
>The first design choice: **keep cards and wires fixed for this pass, or let simpler outlines compete with their placement immediately?** I recommend keeping them fixed first, so we can judge the outline improvement independently.

Since a competition system already exists, I suspect that it will be preferable to engage with this system, but also suspect this is another slider whose weight we can care about more or less, dialing to taste until a solid default is located. I wouldn't even be averse to letting users choose a "Compact" (strongly prefer space-saving) layout or "Geometric" (strongly prefer geometry evenness, element alignment at top or bottom, similarity of grid-like arrangements of nodes, etc.) layout. I bet we could tune the preferences to support one versus the other, and perhaps switching causes the layout bootstrapping to occur once again?
```

I'll probably throw away the export file on that chat since this one starts up a new one. I'd rather keep this all in one coherent `.record.md` for the day if possible.

User: Can we please pursue these compact and geometric presets in 2D **and 3D**? In 2D, what you've achieved is the "Geometric" (or we could call it "Relaxed"? That might gel better if we want to do this in 2D and 3D) preset, functionally.

In 3D, it feels like we're defaulting on the "Relaxed" preset. Why? Because the Force Graph, at least to my knowledge, is being given substantial leeway to optimize the spatial layout all its own with the assumption that any connection carries the same strength. (Please check to see that I'm not making a fool out of myself in this next paragraph hahahaha!)

If the nodes in the force graph were closer to one another as a function of the degree of their coupling (1 connection, 2 connections, etc. might need to sqrt or ln or log10 that number depending on how large we want the range of effect to be), then the emergent shape should likely be a bit more compact. What I don't think I'd want to see are 2 connection lines drawn between 2 nodes if they interact via 2 symbols. I'd much rather see coupling strength represented within space emergently via the force graph simulation (if it allows such a thing). Might also be nice, if possible, to consider sizing the force graph nodes by the number of symbols they expose (and probably sqrt or ln or log10 that, having 1 symbol at a minimum for the "Internals" pseudo-symbol all nodes possess) in addition to the above.

Does this sound like a reasonable UX grammar? Is there perhaps some other way to phrase it? Or perhaps I am trying to force the same "Compact"/"Relaxed" (or "Compact"/"Aesthetic" or "Space-Optimized"/"Layout-Optimized" or some other reasonable semantically opposing name set for the properties we wish to provide leeway around in 2D and 3D) ideas onto settings which would be better served to users as separate things rather than a unified concept that follows between 2D and 3D?

I’d recommend one **Compact / Relaxed** packing preference across both views, with coupling-based proximity and symbol-based sizing retaining the same meaning in either preset.

I also found a counting issue to resolve before using references as a force: one estate pair has three documented relationships, but the Explorer projection represents two of them twice and reports five. We should derive coupling from the underlying graph so display duplication cannot distort the layout.

For the design, should Compact / Relaxed be one packing preference that follows between 2D and 3D, while coupling-based proximity and symbol-based sizing remain consistent across both presets?

- Shared Compact / Relaxed preference; consistent coupling and sizing (recommended)
- Independent layout preferences for 2D and 3D
- Make coupling and sizing separate user-selectable modes

There’s a useful detail for node sizing: the library scales sphere **volume**, so radius already grows by a cube root. Using symbol count directly would therefore give us a compressed size range to inspect before adding another transform.

For the presets, I’d make Compact favor a smaller footprint and Relaxed favor clearance and regularity. More strongly coupled files would tend to sit closer in both; that can reveal clusters, but doesn’t guarantee the entire graph becomes smaller.

Ran terminal command: git status --short; git log -1 --format='%h %s'

Yes. **I recommend a shared Compact / Relaxed preference, with coupling and node size retaining the same meaning in both presets.** Those are related ideas, but they answer different questions.

Your understanding of the current 3D view is substantially right, with a few corrections:

- **One line per file pair already exists.** Parallel symbol references are aggregated; their count currently appears only in the tooltip. It does not affect the simulation. See `fileConnections.ts`.
- **Link strength isn’t literally uniform.** The library weakens links according to their endpoints’ number of neighbors. However, every link has the same default preferred distance, and neither setting uses our symbol-reference count. Your proposed coupling information is missing from the forces. [D3 force documentation](https://github.com/vasturiano/d3-force-3d#link_strength).
- **Files currently have equal base sphere sizes.** Their apparent sizes vary with perspective, but exposed symbols don’t determine their size.

There is also a counting defect to address first: one estate pair has **three documented relationships but five projected links**, because two type relationships are represented twice. Coupling should come from the underlying graph, with an explicit counting rule—not inherit that duplication.

I’d give the shared preference this meaning:

| Perspective | Compact                                                                 | Relaxed                                                                                                         |
| ----------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **2D**      | Favor a smaller footprint while preserving readability and containment. | Spend space when it buys simpler boundaries and better alignment. Today’s default is a good starting candidate. |
| **3D**      | Favor tighter spacing, with enough clearance for each node.             | Allow more breathing room between nodes and clusters.                                                           |

The preference would follow you between perspectives, while each layout uses its own machinery to honor it. **Relaxed** fits this better than _Geometric_; _Aesthetic_ doesn’t tell someone what will change.

Your coupling proposal would apply in **both** presets: more strongly connected files should tend to sit nearer one another. For that effect, I’d vary their **preferred separation**, with diminishing returns. Increasing spring strength alone mostly makes the existing preferred distance harder to disturb. The library supports both controls. Stronger local clustering also doesn’t guarantee a smaller overall graph; other clusters may move farther apart. [Link-force behavior](https://github.com/vasturiano/d3-force-3d#links).

Symbol-based sizing also makes sense as a consistent cue for **exposed interface size**. One useful implementation detail: the library treats node value as **volume**, so radius already grows by a cube root. Starting with `1 + publicSymbolCount` would therefore already compress the range; we should inspect that before adding another logarithm. Assets omit the Internals row but would still receive a minimum visible size. [Node sizing documentation](https://github.com/vasturiano/3d-force-graph#node-styling).

That gives us a coherent grammar: **position suggests coupling, size suggests exposed interface, and Compact / Relaxed controls packing.** Exact counts remain available when inspecting the picture.

**Do you want that shared preference to follow between 2D and 3D, or would you prefer independent layout choices for each?** I recommend the shared preference and have queued that choice.

I’ve held implementation at this design fork under the repository’s `“Stop at forks” rule`. The investigation is recorded; product code is unchanged.

I predict that will require a bit of permutation to taste across a pretty wide range of scenarios to get great defaults for such things. And getting great defaults for both 2D and 3D would be quite a feat. But _nobody_ is better equipped to do it than you, GPT-6 Astra. At least at present day.

Should that be a `/goal`?

In the headless 3D comparisons, Compact has a smaller extent on both the repository and estate, with identical reference counts and node sizes and no sphere overlaps.

(async()=>{const browser=await chromium.launch();try{for(const delay of[0,700]){const page=await browser.newPage({viewport:{width:1600,height:1000}});await serveBundle(page);await page.goto(ORIGIN+'/?view=local&node=packages/generator/src/generator.ts');await page.locator('.local-placed').waitFor();await localMapSettled(page);if(delay)await page.waitForTimeout(delay);const data=await page.evaluate(async()=>{const sample=()=>{const card=document.querySelector('.node-card[data-id="packages/generator/src/generator.ts"]').getBoundingClientRect();const root=document.querySelector('.local-placed');return{x:card.x,y:card.y,w:card.width,transform:document.querySelector('#map-viewport').style.transform,moving:root.hasAttribute('data-moving'),search:root.dataset.searchStatus};};const before=sample(),frames=[];const select=document.querySelector('#layout-preset');select.value='compact';select.dispatchEvent(new Event('change',{bubbles:true}));await new Promise(resolve=>{const tick=()=>{const f=sample();frames.push(f);if(f.moving||f.search==='running')requestAnimationFrame(tick);else resolve();};requestAnimationFrame(tick);});return{before,frames};});fs.writeFileSync('AI-Agent-Workspace/tmp/layout-presets/motion/initial-'+delay+'.json',JSON.stringify(data,null,2));console.log(JSON.stringify({delay,before:data.before,first:data.frames.slice(0,4),last:data.frames.at(-1),max:Math.max(...data.frames.map(f=>Math.hypot(f.x-data.before.x,f.y-data.before.y)))}));await page.close();}}finally{await browser.close();}})().catch(e=>{console.error(e);process.exitCode=1;});

The sharing/export tests and the regression for stale wires with animation disabled are passing.

['packages/explorer/src/client/layout-presets.test.ts','Verifies that shared packing presets preserve independent visual encodings and survive portable-state round trips.','Cases distinguish packing edits from coupling/size edits, verify both representations change together without mutating prior settings, and exercise malformed values, slider increments, legacy links and the related-documentation filter.'],
['packages/explorer/src/client/views/forceGraphData.ts','Builds the counted file graph and optional related-documentation overlay used by both the 3D renderer and the headless spatial report.','Filtering happens before file-pair aggregation. ['packages/explorer/src/client/views/forceGraphData.test.ts','Protects the shared spatial graph from count duplication, mutation and incorrect related-documentation overlays.','Fixtures include weighted reciprocal relationships, object endpoints, repeated documentation citations, live-doc aliases with both path separators, filtered source files and absent source IDs.'],
['packages/explorer/src/client/views/forceGraphLayout.ts','Implements the 3D Local Map’s shared geometry and force settings, including symbol-sized spheres, coupling-dependent gaps and size-aware collision clearance.','Sphere volume grows from one baseline unit plus the documented public-symbol count. A logarithmic coupling term shortens surface gaps, bounded below by collision clearance. The library’s degree-normalized spring strengths are retained. The browser reheats its existing simulation; the headless helper clones inputs and uses the same forces for a deterministic number of ticks. Neither positions nor symbol counts imply runtime call frequency or code complexity.'],
['packages/explorer/src/client/views/forceGraphLayout.test.ts','Exercises the spatial grammar through real deterministic force simulations as well as explicit radius and distance cases.','Tests distinguish sphere volume from radius, maintain size semantics across presets, compare actual settled weak/strong coupling and Compact/Relaxed spacing, and start unequal spheres at coincident positions to exercise clearance rather than merely inspecting the configured formula.'],
['packages/explorer/src/shared/d3-force-3d.ts','Supplies the verified TypeScript contract for the untyped d3-force-3d library used by the Local Map and layout lab.','The installed library publishes no declarations, and no corresponding DefinitelyTyped package was available when this binding was added. This ambient source module covers the API actually used, checked against version 3.0.6; importing it contributes types and emits no implementation. The simulation itself remains the packaged upstream library.'],
['packages/explorer/src/shared/graph.test.ts','Checks that projecting canonical documentation into Explorer links preserves documented multiplicity without duplicating type relationships.','A rendered-and-parsed fixture combines imports, repeated parameter types, return types, inheritance, reciprocal dependencies and an external dependency. Counts are checked against explicit document facts and the canonical graph is required to remain unchanged.'],
['scripts/layout-lab/spatial.ts','Produces the headless 3D Local Map report from the same filtered graph, documented counts and force configuration used in the browser.','A named scope defines retained attention while the full filtered graph supplies forces. Explicit retained files survive test/asset filtering, optional documentation overlays use the shared projection, and missing retained IDs fail explicitly. The report carries settings, counts, radii, positions, preferred and actual pair distances, bounds and sphere overlaps; it is a deterministic tick-based run rather than a claim to reproduce a time-limited live camera session.'],
['scripts/layout-lab/spatial.test.ts','Verifies the spatial report’s graph facts, filtering and geometric semantics against nontrivial fixtures.','Checks summed reciprocal reference counts, known symbol-derived radii and spacing, immutable graph input, explicitly retained test/asset exceptions, documentation overlays and a missing retained file.'],
['tests/e2e/local-map-presets.spec.ts','Keeps the inspected Compact/Relaxed journeys coherent through advanced edits, sharing, exports and motion in both representations.','Runs the same interactions on the repository and estate, preserves visible file/symbol/reference identities, checks coupling and size survive preset changes, and reloads exported/shared settings into a fresh browser context. Motion samples enforce the existing subject-anchor tolerance. A zero-duration estate layout checks actual SVG endpoints against their current pins, guarding the stale-wire defect found during visual tuning.']
];

const notes={
'packages/explorer/src/client/panels/tuning.ts':'The shared Compact/Relaxed selector applies both representations’ packing values. Manual packing changes read as Custom; coupling and size controls survive preset selection. The active perspective’s advanced group opens on a perspective change while either group remains available manually. The returned sync callback keeps controls accurate after navigation. Layout settings can be exported as JSON; the application persists and shares changes and takes over any unfinished camera fit before re-layout.',
'packages/explorer/src/client/persistence/local-storage.ts':'Cold defaults use the shared Relaxed profile. The forceGraph tuning group is merged independently for older stored settings; explicit older numeric preferences remain overrides. Portable packing and spatial values are normalized to the public control ranges and increments before use, so a slider and the layout cannot disagree about the active number.',
'packages/explorer/src/client/views/forceGraphView.ts':'The shared spatial projection supplies counted file pairs and optional related documents. Sphere volume encodes public-symbol count plus a baseline unit; preferred link gaps encode documented coupling with diminishing returns and collision clearance. Packing changes reheat existing positions while keeping the selected screen anchor; unchanged settings do not reheat. Hover labels expose counts. The graph reports its simulation activity with aria-busy, and the renderer’s actual spheres supply perspective-transition sizes.',
'packages/explorer/src/client/views/fileConnections.ts':'A projected edge may represent several documented references sharing endpoints. Pair counts sum that multiplicity (one for older inputs), including reciprocal directions, without multiplying it again through display aliases.',
'packages/explorer/src/shared/graph.ts':'Signature facts remain available as dependency metadata but are emitted as links only once, under their type/inheritance kind. Repeated documented references sharing one visible endpoint/kind pair retain a referenceCount, and resolved target anchors retain canonical symbol identity. This corrects the display duplication that otherwise inflated spatial coupling; the graph index itself is not altered.',
'packages/explorer/src/client/persistence/compressed-url-state.ts':'Optional layout settings carry both representations’ packing and spatial encoding controls, including Custom values. The optional related-documentation flag makes that force-graph input portable too. Earlier links without these fields retain existing preferences. Numeric settings are validated against their control ranges and increments before application.',
'scripts/layout-lab/lab.ts':'Every command accepts --preset compact|relaxed. The spatial command runs the shared D3 forces and graph projection, including optional --tests, --assets and --related-docs filters, then writes Markdown and complete JSON with coordinates, counts and diagnostics. The named scope identifies attention; all filtered files participate in the forces. Refinement, verification and spatial layout accept the page’s exported settings as --config @file.'
};

I’m naming and linking the evidence now, then doing the final documentation checks and commit.

const names={generator:'Generator neighborhood',warehouse:'Warehouse report','repository-five-files':'Repository: five files','repository-chain':'Repository: chain','estate-five-files':'Estate: five files','estate-chain':'Estate: chain'};

const rows=[];let copied=0;

assert.deepEqual(pair.compact.cards,pair.relaxed.cards);assert.deepEqual(pair.compact.refs,pair.relaxed.refs);

for(const key of ['coupling','nodeSize','symbolScale'])assert.equal(fields.compact[key],fields.relaxed[key]);

'| 3D collision clearance | 2 | 5 |',
'',
'Both use coupling 1, base sphere radius 4 and symbol-volume exponent 1. Those independent encodings are not overwritten by choosing another packing preset. Advanced packing edits read Custom. Values are validated against the actual public slider ranges and increments before rendering. Explicit older saved settings remain overrides.',
'',
'## What was compared',
'',
'The [24 headless candidates](tuning-candidates.md), with [complete configurations and measurements](tuning-candidates.json), used the corrected reference projection over the earlier 632-file bundle and the estate. The regularity candidate and refined Compact candidate were then rendered across six scenes: generator, warehouse, repository five files/chain and estate five files/chain. Coupling 0.5 was also inspected on generator and estate; 1 was retained as the starting value.',
'',
'Compact can retain useful shape preferences: increasing its simplicity/evenness from the first provisional settings removed seven dense-repository steps without increasing that picture’s footprint. Relaxed’s stronger regularity removed seven steps there at 61 extra pixels of height and four estate steps while shortening that picture by 90 pixels. These are candidate-study measurements; the new code’s mirrors subsequently changed this repository’s own graph. The final 643-file results are separately preserved in [final-comparisons.json](final-comparisons.json) and the screenshot README.',
'',
'## Final spatial reports',
'',
'All reports use the shared D3 configuration for 300 deterministic ticks. The full filtered graph supplies the forces; the named scope identifies retained attention. The four stock-profile reports show tests and assets, with related documents off. The last report consumes an actual UI-exported custom configuration with assets hidden and related documents enabled.',
'',
'| Report | Files | File pairs | Documented references | Bounds in layout units | Sphere overlaps |',
'| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- | ---: |',
...rows,
'',
'Counts, node identities and intrinsic radii are equal between each stock-profile pair. Compact has a smaller extent in these runs. Browser simulations reheat from the current picture and run under a time limit; these cold tick-based reports are not claims of identical final browser coordinates. Apparent sphere size additionally depends on camera depth.',
'',
'## Meaning and correctness',
'',
'- Imports and linked signature occurrences are counted once each from documented facts. One estate pair previously yielded five display links for three facts because type relationships appeared twice; the projection now removes that duplication and preserves multiplicity on coalesced endpoints. The canonical graph is not filtered or rewritten.',
'- 3D keeps one line per file pair. Hover gives its documented-reference count. A logarithmic coupling term reduces preferred surface separation with diminishing returns, bounded by collision clearance; D3’s neighbor-degree spring-strength rule remains.',
'- Node value represents sphere volume: public-symbol count plus one baseline unit, raised to the chosen exponent. At exponent 1, radius already grows by a cube root. Assets and related documents retain a visible minimum without inventing public symbols.',
'- The shared spatial projection handles related-document overlays identically in the renderer and command, including Live Doc aliases, filtered source files and reciprocal links.',
'- Exported settings describe packing and spatial encodings, not a camera snapshot or a unique settled arrangement. Warm and cold starts can differ. The page can restore these settings from a shared URL, and the lab accepts the exported JSON as "'`--config @file`.'"',
'',
'## Findings corrected during the work',
'',
'Zero-duration placement could move cards without redrawing their wires; it now redraws the actual endpoints and holds the subject. A preset applied during unfinished automatic framing could move the selected file about five pixels; explicit tuning now takes over that camera before re-layout, without relaxing the existing 1.5-pixel regression bound. Final motion samples found about 0.001036 pixels of 2D drift and zero 3D anchor drift.',
'',
'Two older browser journeys assumed the previous on-screen geometry or a single Tuning disclosure. They now use the existing public pan-and-click helper and the actual Tuning control. Their original semantic assertions and timeouts remain. A separate read-only collision check placed unequal unlinked spheres together with zero charge: they separated to 37.5 units against a required clearance of 25, exercising collision independently of link or repulsion forces.',
'',
'## Verification and reproducibility',
'',
'"'`npm run safe:commit -- --e2e` passed 988 unit tests, 52 integration tests and all 104 browser tests, plus lint, builds, test type-checking, complete regeneration/lint and all documentation, asset and symbol audits. The final generator pass found all 643 mirrors unchanged. No product source changed after the successful gate. The [root session record](../../../ChatHistory/2026/10/2026-10-13.1.record.md) retains intermediate failures and the final closeout.'"',
'',
'Example commands:',
'',
'"'`sh'"',
'npm run layout:lab -- spatial repository/five --preset compact',
'npm run layout:lab -- spatial estate/five --preset relaxed',
'npm run layout:lab -- spatial repository/five --config @local-map-layout.json --assets false --related-docs true',
'npm run layout:lab -- capture estate/five --preset compact',
'npm run layout:lab -- refine estate/five --preset compact --config @local-map-layout.json',
'"'`'"',
'',
'Build the repository and estate bundles first. The lab’s page captures and intermediate scripts are disposable; the named pictures, reports, candidate configurations and unique findings are retained here.',
''
];fs.writeFileSync(path.join(dir,'README.md'),content.join('\n'));console.log('Wrote final probe findings and linked all five spatial reports.');
NODE

Ran terminal command: git status --short && git log -2 --format='%h %s'

Implemented in commit `5fc95351`; **the goal is complete**.

- **Compact / Relaxed follows you between 2D and 3D.** Compact favors tighter packing; Relaxed allows simpler boundaries and alignment in 2D, and more breathing room in 3D.
- **3D expresses coupling through distance and exposed interface through node size**, with one line per file pair.
- **Advanced controls remain available.** Packing edits show “Custom”; switching presets preserves coupling and sizing preferences. Settings persist, travel in shared links, and export for headless comparisons.
- Preset changes preserve your selected subject’s position.

I compared 24 candidate configurations and inspected the final presets across six scenarios. The `visual comparisons and motion captures` document the resulting defaults.

**Full verification passed:** 988 unit, 52 integration, and 104 browser tests, plus builds and documentation audits.

The `daily record` remains coherent. The only uncommitted change is the independently appended `chat export`, preserved intact. The Explorer is open with Relaxed selected.

Goal elapsed time: about three hours.

User: >The alignment question has a real tradeoff: the solver also pays for wire length and space reserved for wires passing another column.

Oooooh! I love being wrong in these ways. That's so great. Yeah, it's possible that the alternative I described is _not_ better, even to the human eye. But the signal I was getting is that "Relaxed" could afford to permit a little more connector length (penalize less? weigh lower connector length with less intensity?) for the benefit of more points of alignment, better layout composition, etc. Hence the plausible phrase "Layout-Optimized" for that "Relaxed" setting.

The following browser pages are currently shared with you and can be interacted with using the browser tools:

- [9da66710-4cce-4e3b-a58d-a0462823bb5a] Live Docs Explorer (http://127.0.0.1:8901/?view=local&mode=2d&node=tests%2Fintegration%2Fprograms%2Ftypescript%2Frosetta%2Fsrc%2Fmain.ts) (active)

1 page is open but not shared.
Use the 'open_browser_page' tool to open a new page or to help the user share an existing page.

Codex: No need to stop your hosting terminal. I’ll reproduce these on port 8901, trace the dimming, zoom and connector-motion problems, and compare the three-card layout’s actual costs. That should show where alignment is losing and whether the missing ancestors reflect a rendering bug or the current selection behavior.

- **Current work:** the owner-review corrections in Turns 7–8 are implemented, visually inspected and fully verified. Final source passed the third complete '"\"'`safe:commit -- --e2e'"\"'`: 990 unit tests, 52 integration tests, all 108 browser tests, builds/type/lint and all audits; all 643 mirrors regenerate unchanged. No verification process remains running. Standing-authorized staging/commit is next; check git if this closeout was interrupted. Pre-feedback HEAD is '"\"'`41abcd43'"\"'`.
- **Implemented:** Compact now values alignment at 0.25 and Relaxed at 2. Matching SVG wires keep their elements and hover state; entering wires fade with their endpoint cards; frame redraws do not restart layer opacity. Both viewports clear transient hover on exit. 3D hover temporarily reveals incident connections and recovers when the same sphere is re-entered, using the existing direct raycast. Wheel input cannot reverse direction to enforce a distance limit crossed through another input method. Perspective captures retain current wire presence.
- **Evidence:** [the feedback report](../../../Probes/2026-10-13/local-map-feedback/README.md) and [34 named browser pictures](../../../Screenshots/2026-10-13/feedback/README.md) preserve the comparisons and motion. The both-bypasses-below image is a separately solved illustration; the shipping ordering search is unchanged. The [five owner images](2026-10-13.1.images/README.md) are byte-preserved. Stronger Relaxed alignment was compared over seven scenes, Compact over four.

Implemented and committed as `81450b3f`.

**Relaxed now spends more on alignment and composition.** Compact also gives alignment a small positive weight. Your three-card scene aligns in Relaxed for about two extra pixels of wire. Presets still change spacing, so switching can move an otherwise similar arrangement.

I also fixed:

- **3D hover**, including leaving and re-entering the same sphere.
- **Reversed wheel movement** after middle-button zoom crosses the wheel’s distance limit.
- **Connector flicker:** matching wires retain their elements, and new wires fade with their cards.

The tested normal, zoomed and interrupted transitions keep endpoints attached. I haven’t reproduced your particular detachment sequence yet. Your both-bypasses-underneath arrangement remains a `comparison candidate`.

**Verification:** 990 unit, 52 integration and 108 browser tests passed. `Pictures and measurements` are saved. Keep your server running; refresh port 8901 and reselect the preset.

One distinction remains: opened directories retain attention after file pins are cleared. Ancestor chains are currently compressed; clicking a membrane opens its contents.

**Should “Clear pins” also count and clear opened directories?** I recommend yes.

I’ve held that behavior change and ancestor navigation at the `AGENTS.md` rule: “Stop at forks.”

User: This is a phenomenal improvement. It's been a joy to go back and forth from 2D and 3D and relaxed to compact.

> One distinction remains: opened directories retain attention after file pins are cleared. Ancestor chains are currently compressed; clicking a membrane opens its contents.

The next thing I'd like to have at my disposal is pin management. I can tell as the tool becomes increasingly useful that I want to dispose of old pins as I spider my way around. So the "1 pin x" is a great start but I think I'm going to need to be able to get a stack-like list of pins drawn so that I can release the old ones on the bottom as I finish seeing what I need. I can tell that I have a need to preserve one or more pins along the way. I'd like to be able to dismiss the "encasing" state of directories as well which are irrelevant. Preferably, I'd be able to see directories, nodes, and symbols which have active pinning engaged. Or at least some list on the right (as a outgrowth of the "1 pin", which should probably separate into its own lower-right element that can be collapsed/hidden) of things which _can be dismissed/hidden/compacted_. So long as we take the "Relaxed/2D/3D" and move that to the bottom center in its own element, we shooould be okay? I will say though that Relaxed and Compact should be icons with tooltips for their names. A dropdown seems inappropriate there unless we want that little dropdown to be the surface by which we drill-in as developers and make little tweaks to the rendering to make it perfect.

Can you envision a way this can be grokked with the overall design? Or do you feel like there are some gotchas that you would prefer to push back on first?

> **Should “Clear pins” also count and clear opened directories?** I recommend yes.

Yes but we do perhaps run into weirdness when we get down to the root where there's really not much to click on and it's hard to know where to start. Do you think it might be wiser for the unpin-all state to just display the root directory or something? I'm not sure. I've gone back and forth on whether I want the entire membrane stack visible at all times all the way up to the root. Sometimes I'm in favor and sometimes I'm against.

The main gotcha is that an encasing membrane may have no pin of its own: it exists because a retained connection needs a file inside it. Releasing an opened folder can remove its extra contents, but some files and their enclosure may still be needed.

The tray can make that distinction visible. Clearing everything can also leave a useful place to start without silently pinning the whole repository again.

For an encasing directory whose files are still needed by another pin, what should “dismiss” do? I recommend treating its outline separately from the retained connections.

- Hide the directory outline; keep the required files and wires visible.
- Remove that branch from view, even if another pin still connects to it.
- Only release explicit directory pins; keep required enclosures visible.

Ran terminal command: git status --short; git log -1 --oneline

Yes. I’d make **pins a visible working set**, shared between 2D and 3D.

| Location        | Proposed behavior                                                                                                                               |
| --------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Bottom center   | Compact and Relaxed icon buttons, then **2D / 3D**. Tooltips name the presets; a sliders button opens Tuning and indicates customized settings. |
| Bottom right    | A **Pins · n** handle that opens or collapses the tray.                                                                                         |
| Inside the tray | Newest selections at the top, oldest at the bottom. Directory, file and symbol entries each have an individual release action.                  |

Clicking an entry’s name would bring it into view without changing its pins or its position in the list. A whole-file pin stays one entry; individual symbol selections can expand beneath their file. Releasing a directory should preserve anything inside it that you pinned separately. The tray would also coordinate with the existing Details drawer so they don’t cover each other.

**The important gotcha is that releasing something doesn’t always make it disappear.** Another pin may still need that file and its connections. The tray should explain that with a contextual “Needed by…” indication, rather than leave you wondering whether the release worked.

Your agreement settles **Clear pins including opened directories**. For the resulting zero-pin state, I recommend:

- **2D:** show the scan root’s immediate files and closed child directories—one level deep, ready to explore.
- **3D:** restore normal visibility throughout the filtered graph, preserving camera orientation.

That root display would be a browsing fallback with **zero pins**. It wouldn’t silently retain the repository or keep adding unrelated contents once you start another exploration.

For ancestors, I’d keep the compressed membranes by default and provide a clickable ancestor path. That gives you access to the root without requiring every enclosing boundary to occupy the picture.

The remaining distinction is **dismissing an encasing directory**. I recommend separating “hide this outline” from “release this pin.” Hiding an outline can preserve its files and wires; removing its connected contents changes what the picture tells you and needs an explicit indication of omitted connections.

**When you say dismiss an encasing directory, do you mean remove its outline, or get its files and connections out of view too?**

I’ve recorded the proposal and kept implementation at this choice because `AGENTS.md` says “Stop at forks”; those two meanings require different behavior.

Yes, in Unix terms, a directory is a special kind of file. And the analogy is useful here: **a source file contains symbols; a directory contains files.** The ambiguity comes from what selecting a container means for selections inside it.

Your answer gives us a sound default: **release this selection, then reduce the picture as far as the remaining selections and connections allow.**

For example: you open a directory, pin one file inside it, and another file is needed by an outside pin. Releasing the directory removes its extra browsing contents. Those two files remain, with their enclosure. If the directory already contains only required things, there is nothing further to release.

I’d distinguish these operations explicitly:

| Action                               | Meaning                                                                                        |
| ------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Release directory**                | Remove its own retained opening; preserve independent selections inside it.                    |
| **Clear pins inside this directory** | Deliberately clear the contained selections too. Outside pins can still require some contents. |

That second action is a useful bulk operation to consider. It needn’t become the meaning of every ×. This also revises my earlier outline-hiding suggestion: ordinary release should follow the retention rule you described.

For ancestors, **I meant making the path already printed on the outermost membrane individually clickable**—schematically, `root › … › parent › current`. It would sit on that membrane’s upper edge. The ellipsis exposes intermediate ancestors; choosing one opens its immediate contents.

Your thin enclosing membranes are also a plausible presentation. The constraint is their labels and click targets: six thin outlines are cheap, but six readable, independently clickable names still need space. A combination could work well: one readable ancestor path, with hovering or keyboard-focusing an ancestor temporarily showing its thin enclosing outline. We should compare that against persistent thin wrappers by eye.

There’s also a useful correction about 3D: **directory retention already exists there**. Opened directories contribute their member files to the `3D attention calculation`. What’s missing is a visible directory object and a convenient release control.

The shared tray supplies that control. An opened directory can remain a named entry in either representation, and its × can release the same retained request. We can establish that shared behavior before deciding what a directory should look like in 3D.

User: >One correction: existing directory membership controls 3D dimming and framing; it has not been an attraction force. The new directory nodes will add that influence.

Noted; appreciated

The first 3D comparison shows 18 directory hubs in the estate sample and 236 in this repository. Toggling membership lines changed no node positions or camera coordinates in either settled scene.

The visual check caught a framing issue: clicking a directory approached its hub too closely to keep its files in view. I’ve changed that action to frame the hub and its members together, and I’m checking the result.

The focused browser run passed 26 of 28 tests. One failure still expected closing a parent to close its independently opened child—the behavior we’ve deliberately changed. The other was a 30-second timeout in the generator preset journey; I’ll rerun that on its own without changing the limit.

I also found why directory framing was still wrong: the library’s fit operation aims at the world origin, even with filtered bounds. I’m replacing that calculation with one centered on the selected members while preserving the viewing direction.

const fs = require('node:fs');
const path = require('node:path');
const record = 'AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/10/2026-10-13.1.record.md';
const docs = [
['packages/explorer/src/client/panels/icon-button.ts', 'Creates named SVG action buttons for the Local Map’s compact controls.', 'The caller supplies application-owned SVG drawing markup and a human-readable name; the helper supplies the button type, tooltip and accessible label. Action state and behavior remain with the caller. The shared glyphs keep release and directory actions recognizable across the tray and membrane controls.'],
['packages/explorer/src/client/panels/pin-tray.ts', 'Makes retained directories, files and symbols individually reachable and releasable in either Local Map representation.', 'Rows use stable selection identities and appear newest first. Updating the list preserves surviving elements and transfers keyboard focus when the selected row disappears. A whole-file selection is one entry; directory bulk clearing is a separately named action. The tray delegates all selection changes to the application, collapses when Details opens and keeps its handle available. Its order comes from the shared exploration snapshot, rather than from the arrangement of cards on screen.'],
['packages/explorer/src/client/views/localView/directory-ancestry.ts', 'Provides ancestor navigation attached to a directory membrane without adding a permanent row of breadcrumb labels to the map.', 'The disclosure opens in the browser’s top layer at viewport text size, so it stays usable when the diagram is zoomed out. A short stack of ancestor buttons opens the selected directory through the ordinary retention operation. Hover/focus previews an enclosure around currently drawn members only; it makes no claim to show every file in that ancestor. Escape and outside clicks dismiss the disclosure and remove the preview.'],
['packages/explorer/src/client/views/retained-state.ts', 'Defines independent selection release, explicit directory bulk clearing and a common presentation order for the Local Map’s retained working set.', 'The file/symbol pin set and opened-directory set remain the sources of retention. Ordered identities are presentation metadata: surviving entries keep their position, new selections append and stale or duplicate identities are discarded. A directory release removes only that opening; bulk clearing removes selections throughout its subtree. References required by outside pins remain the responsibility of the shared graph-disclosure functions. Older links without a common chronology receive a deterministic fallback order.'],
['packages/explorer/src/client/views/retained-state.test.ts', 'Guards independent container release, subtree clearing and portable ordering of retained selections.', 'The counterexamples include an independently opened child, similarly prefixed sibling directories, an outside pin that still needs a released file, repinning after release and stale shared-link entries. These assertions protect selection meaning rather than the tray’s DOM arrangement.'],
['tests/e2e/force-graph-observation.ts', 'Observes the native Three.js meshes and camera used by the spatial-view browser regressions.', 'The helper listens to Three’s development-tool event inside the test page, then reads rendered node identity, material opacity, world and screen positions, and link kinds/visibility. This lets tests compare what reaches WebGL instead of accepting an application-maintained diagnostic as evidence of its own correctness. It introduces no product endpoint or browser-global application API. The original observation lived in the force-graph focus regression; directory-hub checks need the same measurements.'],
['tests/e2e/local-map-pins.spec.ts', 'Exercises pin management as browser journeys across sharing, directory release, root browsing and ancestor navigation.', 'The tests protect newest-first ordering and surviving DOM identities, independent child selections, external connections after subtree clearing, an actually unpinned root after reload, and legible ancestor controls at reduced diagram scale. Text collision and truncation checks cover the new tray and disclosure after their visual inspection.']
];
for (const [source,purpose,notes] of docs) {
const file = "'`.mdmd/layer-4/${source}.mdmd.md`;
const link = path.relative(path.dirname(file),record).split(path.sep).join('"'/')+'#turn-11--pin-management-approved-ancestor-disclosure-and-directory-hubs';
let text = fs.readFileSync(file,'utf8');
if ("'!text.includes('"'_Pending authored purpose_') || "'!text.includes('"'_Pending notes_')) throw new Error("'`Unexpected authored template: ${file}`);
text = text.replace('"'_Pending authored purpose_', purpose).replace('_Pending notes_', notes+"'`'"\\n\\nIntroduced for the owner’s [October 14 pin-management request]("'${link}).`);
fs.writeFileSync(file,text);
}
console.log(`Wrote authored sections for ${docs.length} new source mirrors; generated regions unchanged.`);
NODE'
